### BW-P4-0059 - Native Outset presentation and control
- Category: NATIVE_OUTSET_PRESENTATION_CONTROL; state: active; priority: S1;

  `fc3fb529bd642bd2da0498eb49dcf27e73d8d9b98dd2838311d302ce90f36ba0`.
- EFB copy-cache lifecycle result: supported and promoted, but falsified as the
  complete visual owner. The live Aurora path keys copy allocations by guest
  destination, width, height, and format, then maps the destination to the most
  recent copy. gxcore formerly keyed only by destination. The expanded

  same destination with a 320x240 RGBA8 copy; the old route returns quadrant

  635,401,090 blocks/retrace 14,200 with 1,302,398 failure-free submissions, but
  changes zero synchronized pixels: 761,938 nonblank, framebuffer hash
  `0xE866EA294402E9CA`, PPM SHA-256
  `f8a689d35335cc5005b34825a35f144a2f5a1c6df96dbe44cd64ef865ba1cf04`,
  log SHA-256
  `479b19c863ee2d47176f1ff8342fa16a4916628cbd45168ea6e11a9477340508`.
- Room 44 material rejection: direct MDL3/VTX1 decoding shows all eight terrain
  materials use one CMPR texture, two ordinary TEV stages, white fog, unlit

  corrected observer records no active nonzero-mask lit draw after frame 7,140.
  TEV combiner parsing, fog saturation, and active light arithmetic do not own

- Residual test risk: an earlier early-depth Metal fixture trapped in Dawn's

  `003621286218d25a99ba396e36fcea4a57f7cf9c55934da6fd28f32788f5e034`).
  It passes when rerun in the serial six-fixture gate, and both native game

- Rejected Step-0 candidate: missing XF dual-texture/post-matrix delivery is not
  admitted as this scene's owner. The common J3D world path writes
  `GX_PTIDENTITY` for every texgen, and the opening trace has no post-matrix
  loads; implementing it now would lack an authentic use oracle.
- Reframed next action: return to Step 0 and select a new donor-backed semantic
  mismatch outside sampler, coordinate, framebuffer-depth clear, scaled-copy,
  copy-cache lifecycle, rejected Room 44 TEV/fog/light state, and unused
  post-matrix state.
  Begin with source/state comparison, then a failing pure or GPU oracle; do not
  move the capture boundary or add another aggregate trace.
- Prohibited: forced scenes, guest-state writes, fixed-retrace input, renderer
  bypasses presented as native evidence, a second emulator, or heap/ARAM
  tracing.
- No simulator used.

## Resolved
### BW-P4-0058 - Raw and linked REL addresses lack an unambiguous representation
- Category: REL_ADDRESS_REPRESENTATION; state: resolved; priority: S1; owner:
  composite generator linked-address namespace plus authentic OSLink
  publication.
- First seen: after natural opening completion reached authentic `PLAY_SCENE`
  on 2026-08-27.
- Gate/content: P4 milestone 6, Outset room/player creation by original actor
  code in the native macOS host.
- Stable starting signature: state 44/`opening-complete` occurs at retrace
  13,961 and `PLAY_SCENE` at 14,021. The original play scene mounts its stage
  and object archives and materializes actor RELs through `d_a_npc_so`, then
  reports `archived resource unavailable name="d_a_npc_md"` and stops at
  unmapped PC `0x8040CE60` after block 740,358,454 in the corrected complete
  replay.
- Falsified subhypothesis: the resource is not missing or malformed.
  `generated/full/rels/d_a_npc_md.rel` exists, is 119,120 bytes, and has the
  expected REL header/module ID 147.
- Falsified allocator hypothesis: owner-aware slot reclamation plus bounded
  first-fit placement does not materialize `d_a_npc_md`. The complete replay
  reaches the same terminal because 102 eagerly installed fixed REL data/BSS
  ranges occupy the scratch window. Even after removing every dynamic slot,
  the largest free gap is 113,648 bytes (`0x1BBF0`) at
  `[0x81E021B0,0x81E1DDA0)`, smaller than the aligned 119,136-byte
  (`0x1D160`) request. Dynamic-slot reclamation cannot repair that inequality.
- Falsified lazy-alias hypothesis: installing fixed data/BSS aliases only for
  resident modules does free raw scratch, but it also lets a raw executable
  slot occupy a future linked AOT code address. In the 65M control,
  `d_a_ship` raw executable code began at `0x81E000F4`; later `d_a_title`
  entered linked code at `0x81E000DC`. The same numeric PC then denoted both
  modules. Constructor traversal repeatedly re-entered ship lifecycle code at
  table `0x81E0DAF0`/target `0x81E0D414`, held retrace 330, and never reached
  title-ready. Reserving the incoming module's own data ranges did not change
  this cross-module executable collision.
- Falsified in-MEM1 publication hypothesis: canonicalizing executable section
  offsets before authentic OSLink and retaining the retail constructor table
  image removes the ship/title ambiguity and reaches exact title-ready retrace
  330, but the first title render reads a zero vtable from `0x815581FA`.
  That address is inside generated module-60 BSS while the owning material at
  `0x815550C0` is inside module-60's invented code range; both are simultaneously
  live retail heap addresses. No-input and BSS-only residency controls reproduce
  the same terminal, so neither input nor alias lifetime owns the failure.
- Falsifiable hypothesis: assigning every generated REL code/data/BSS section
  to a deterministic virtual aperture disjoint from retail MEM1, and publishing
  those addresses through authentic OSLink, will remove raw/linked and
  heap/linked numeric ambiguity while preserving exact title retrace and
  allowing raw staging to reuse retail storage independently.
- Bounded hypothesis result: supported. All 415 RELs were regenerated in the
  tagged aperture `0xC04000F4..0xC1F80170`; generator namespace contracts,
  translator/runtime tests, and baseline/candidate ABI coverage pass. The
  first title qualification exposed one integration defect: REL code called
  the DOL lifecycle API as tagged PC `0xC0241178`, but the pre-dispatch host
  bridge recognized only canonical `0x80241178`. Canonicalizing that comparison
  removed the prior block-14,032,201 `0x4182002C` constructor terminal. The
  corrected candidate stops normally at 15M and 25M blocks after modules 168
  and 400 and continued room/actor creation. This admits the candidate to the
  complete tier; it does not resolve P4 milestone 6 because title-ready and the
  full donor-backed opening-to-Outset route have not yet been requalified.
  Evidence SHA-256: `b85330b79bf6756a4882ae96384d1a36a56bec844a2e860edd1cdde93fd642f1`
  and `aa3bdf016d0a392e8f558b020a693157f8f8a06753251386b1543e9955e5871f`.
- Extended qualification result: supported through original file select. The
  first 800M no-input tagged run did not create title, proving that the 25M
  normal stop had not exercised the title profile. Exact tracing found that
  startup installed tagged aliases for BSS but not file-backed REL data; those
  writes fell through to the masked raw staging image and dynamic loading later
  overwrote the title profile. Aliasing every linked storage section restored
  profile 449 and authentic title execution. The next title-confirm route
  exposed a second shared metadata mismatch: BlueWake placed `d_a_kanban` BSS
  at `0xC09A4480` using fixed 32-byte alignment, while generated code referenced
  `0xC09A4470`. The REL declares 8-byte BSS alignment, and DolRecomp uses that
  header field with a four-byte fallback. The composite generator now applies
  the identical rule in both metadata paths. A corrected 65M/700-retrace run
  clears the former block-40,362,071 destructor terminal and stops normally at
  block 59,696,337. It reaches title-ready/retrace 333,
  name-scene-create/retrace 451, name-scene-execute and memcard-check/retrace
  459, file-select/retrace 649, and the new-file-slot event/retrace 690.
  Evidence SHA-256:
  `ebe045d50c6e6135ac1788717d437972a329dfb0fede0468154a21508173680a`.
  This strongly qualifies the representation through file creation but does
  not yet resolve milestone 6; opening completion, `PLAY_SCENE`, and progress
  beyond `d_a_npc_md` still require the full authentic tier.
- Inconclusive early control: the first post-fix replay retained the host's
  default 50,000,000-block ceiling and therefore stopped normally at file
  select/retrace 645 before exercising this blocker. It confirms no early boot,
  DVD, ARAM, DSP, or title regression but cannot qualify REL reclamation.
  Evidence SHA-256:
  `c379d69f2c2e414588321526e9575b88fd0e26a6f41338fd180436702c701c64`.
- Exact authentic reproducer: `BLUEWAKE_DOL=generated/full/main.dol`, private
  `GZLE01-disc-image`, `BLUEWAKE_RELS_DIR=generated/full/rels`, pinned donor
  IROM/COEF, headless renderer, `BLUEWAKE_PAD_BUTTONS=0x0100`,
  `BLUEWAKE_PAD_PULSE_ON_TITLE_READY=1`, two-retrace event length,
  `BLUEWAKE_MAX_BLOCKS=800000000`, and `BLUEWAKE_MAX_RETRACES=14200`, using
  `build/runtime-host-dsp/bluewake_host` with the canonical composite dylib.
- Profile-backed control correction: the first correctly bounded replay was
  intentionally stopped after matching opening state 9/retrace 1,699 and 115M
  blocks because a native sample attributed 1,546/2,516 main-thread samples to
  donor DSP scheduling, dominated by trace-only `getenv()` and observational
  register reads. `fe95020` caches trace configuration, removes those reads
  only from trace-off execution, and makes the formerly `NDEBUG`-disabled DSP
  tests execute in Release. Trace-off, trace-on, and all 17 runnable contracts
  pass. Partial replay SHA-256:
  `19f3fe1df3d8c113bd7c8bbf4bc11edb559c993ed9081bf2841d1297d92dcf12`.
  A second profile of its replacement attributed 3,022/4,202 top-of-stack
  samples to repeated diagnostic `getenv()` locking. Commit `f5fdfdb` caches
  those immutable flags at startup. The 65M-block qualification preserves
  every observed milestone and exact block count through the opening request
  while completing in 301.64 seconds, about 4.6x faster. Its SHA-256 is
  `2e2728281a57adf564f02e2837d2022fafa8e126c96274e1aaa3daf79fc6f5e1`.
  A post-cache sample then placed 3,552/4,202 top-of-stack samples in the
  1,901-entry guest-alias scan. Commit `3845fb7` adds aggregate alias bounds
  without changing first-match semantics. Its 65M-block equivalence run
  preserved every exact milestone in 120.99 seconds; SHA-256:
  `4d7335249c3c465c90ddafa2c8ca01b7ac37b713f70583d410fe6ac601e804ff`.
  The final replacement replay completed in 1,178.66 seconds, reproduced state
  42/retrace 13,931, state 44/retrace 13,961, and `PLAY_SCENE`/retrace 14,021,
  then stopped at the same `d_a_npc_md` boundary after block 740,358,454.
  It recorded 225 DVD forwards with zero failures, 4,431 ARAM transfers with
  zero rejects, and 843,955 donor DSP DMAs. Evidence SHA-256:
  `87d2508722259b624949c0c572ae45e3aa6aad94adcbef93e4eb3170d872ecb5`.
- Failed-candidate evidence: lazy lifecycle 65M SHA-256
  `97e747278c6c7eb9d54ccb5f5a41fce3604e0f4ede10e95dee41d144ec57aad4`;
  one bounded lifecycle trace SHA-256
  `8eaeccdc8d925f46741efa25bd676f16e19679282cfa93e5a18500dc03df798b`.
  Commit `1287233` reverts candidate `650ef04`. Its 25M recovery control
  restores exact title-ready retrace 330/block 20,371,066; SHA-256
  `54ba1bd3cc906bfc0fc4e17fa6270a4b46df0838138770442420169f0d5a88a3`.
- Second-candidate evidence: terminal-state SHA-256
  `2a72f08736701f85c6e1339e77e6015469ac0afe64d171b5663b67f2b0f180f9`;
  BSS-residency falsification SHA-256
  `5ee67497a63da815ef8b9c310e23440fe1650764a23409c0a49a8475a502225a`.
  The candidate was removed without commit and the recovery tree restored.
- Resolution: the complete tagged 800M/14,200-retrace route reaches opening
  state 42/retrace 13,935, opening-complete/retrace 13,965, and
  `PLAY_SCENE`/retrace 14,025. It materializes `d_a_npc_md` at raw
  `0x81E48540`, continues through many later actor RELs, mounts
  `Stage/sea/Room44.arc`, and completes overlap phase 6/retrace 14,098. The run
  stops normally at 800M blocks/retrace 14,163 with zero DVD failures and zero
  ARAM rejects. This passes the exact promotion criterion and resolves the
  address-representation blocker. Evidence SHA-256:
  `c32082d4d0dac64fa254ac9efc936183a01983ec87cbddfd9d43343d318db668`.
- Prohibited: module-name special cases, expanding the scratch window, moving
  live REL images, fixed-retrace input, guest-state writes, forced scene
  construction, a second emulator, or heap/ARAM boundary tracing.
- No simulator used.

### BW-P4-0057 - Authentic opening-to-Outset scene progression
- Category: SCENE_PROGRESSION; state: resolved; priority: S1; owner: retail
  opening-scene transition, play-scene creation, and original room/actor load.
- First seen: after native opening presentation passed on 2026-08-26.
- Gate/content: P4 milestone 6, Outset loaded by original scene/actor code in
  the native macOS host.
- Stable starting signature: the authentic donor-backed signed app visibly
  presents original opening narration at retrace 1083 and stops normally at
  retrace 1200 with progression, audio, ARAM, and GX invariants coherent.
- Falsified subhypothesis: the A/B/Start skip in `dScnOpen_c::execute` applies
  only to `OPEN2_SCENE` (`0x000F`). A new game creates `OPEN_SCENE` (`0x000E`),
  whose source-defined route completes naturally at state 44 and changes to
  `PLAY_SCENE`. The first transition observer targeted the wrong process and
  was stopped rather than extended.
- Falsifiable hypothesis: natural retail state progression will reach state 44,
  request `PLAY_SCENE`, and load Outset's `sea_T/Room44.arc` plus original room
  and player actors without host scene intervention.
- Decisive timing result: one corrected donor-backed headless run progresses
  naturally through states 0-25 and stops normally at retrace 5500/block
  304,753,231. Long message states 3, 9, 13, and 19 complete at retraces 1545,
  2663, 4217, and 5423; states 20-25 then advance through retrace 5485. The
  terminal summaries remain coherent: 211/211 JAS forwards, 4,310 ARAM
  transfers with zero rejects, and 381,368 donor DSP DMAs. State 44,
  `PLAY_SCENE`, and qualified Outset loading are not reached. Evidence
  SHA-256:
  `e06c3225ad8cc186578591b2f21be5a387ee0130769b3b2625c8a833556a0ffe`.
- Oracle correction: `sea_T/Room44.arc` is preloaded during boot at retrace
  286, so pathname alone is not an Outset milestone. It must occur after the
  observer has seen opening state 44, followed by `PLAY_SCENE` and original
  room/player creation.
- Input-contract result: translated `dScnOpen_message_c::exec`
  (`0x8023334C-0x80233524`) contains no PAD/controller call. Combined with the
  scene-level `OPEN2_SCENE`-only skip check, this proves the new-game
  `OPEN_SCENE` must complete naturally.
- Static owner map: `dScnOpen_proc_c::proc_execute` defines four remaining
  message waits at states 25 (`0x580`), 29 (`0x585`), 35 (`0x589`), and 41
  (`0x58a`). All intervening states are bounded alpha transitions or 60/90-tick
  timers, and state 43 fades directly to state 44.
- Extended control result: states 25, 29, and 35 complete naturally at
  retraces 8184, 10476, and 11156; state 41 begins at 11310 and remains active
  at the normal retrace-12500/block-661,402,101 stop. The terminal summaries
  remain coherent: 211/211 JAS forwards, 4,310 ARAM transfers with zero
  rejects, and 761,330 donor DSP DMAs. Evidence SHA-256:
  `64f38eec37a8018a23baeac90d7f2c01f18cfdc5a4f225dc59d93cc67db2c328`.
- Falsified bound assumption: state 25 lasted 2,699 retraces, exceeding the
  earlier 1,462-retrace maximum. A maximum extrapolated from earlier messages
  is not a source-backed oracle.
- Retail-data timing oracle: `set_message(id, n)` stores `n-1` at message
  offset `0x22ec` and `exec` chains consecutive IDs. The DOL delay table byte
  for each ID is multiplied by 30 scene ticks, or 60 VI retraces at the
  opening's 30 Hz rate. Every completed chain matches
  `sum(delay * 60) + 46 + 138 * (n - 1)` within one retrace. State 41's
  `set_message(0x58a, 4)` consumes IDs `0x58a..0x58d`; their authentic table
  bytes are `9,10,10,7`, predicting state 42 at retrace 13,930 and state 44
  near 13,960.
- Resolution: the bounded authentic control reaches state 42 at retrace
  13,931, state 44/`opening-complete` at 13,961, and `PLAY_SCENE` at 14,021.
  This confirms the retail BMG/DOL timing oracle within one retrace and proves
  natural opening-to-play progression. Outset room/player qualification moves
  to `BW-P4-0058` because the next stop belongs to REL lifecycle, not scene
  progression. Evidence SHA-256:
  `3aa843f332b09dc63a889d80a2737c0eeaca6a9a17d5a8044eea9bb2afae1fe1`.
- Prohibited: fixed-retrace input, direct guest-state writes, forced scene
  construction, a second emulator, or reviving heap/ARAM boundary tracing.
- No simulator used.

### BW-P4-0056 - Native macOS presentation of the opening scene
- Category: PRESENTATION; state: resolved; priority: S1; owner: native Aurora/GX
  route with donor DSP and retail-state synchronized input.
- First seen: post-headless opening-scene qualification on 2026-08-26.
- Gate/content: visible, stable original opening scene in the native macOS host.
- Stable signature: the authentic donor-backed native route reaches overlap
  phase 6 at retrace 947 and then produces 51 identically black samples through
  retrace 1049 (`0x5DE270C51C460383`) while submitting 74-75 draws per frame.
  It stops normally with all progression/audio/resource invariants coherent.
- Measured control: a default-GXCore native run captured 134,935 nonblack
  pixels at 1920x1440. The logo's normalized bounds match Dolphin dumped frame
  66, and its dimmer red is consistent with an earlier point in the same fade.
  This rules out a globally black or disconnected native presentation path in
  the adapter-inactive control only.
- Falsifiable hypothesis: the native donor-backed route will follow the same
  event-synchronized milestones and visibly present the opening scene without
  a renderer-specific stall or crash.
- Anti-stall response: three captures selected by guessed temporal boundaries
  exhausted that experiment shape. The asynchronous observer now re-arms after
  black frames and records the first nonblack framebuffer without changing
  guest state, rendering, input, timing, or scheduling.
- Completed adapter-inactive control: the bounded replay stops normally at
  retrace 1050 with
  all progression milestones, 211/211 JAS forwards, zero ARAM rejects, and no
  GXCore rejection/failure. Evidence log SHA-256:
  `8f1932a8ee6566b12be022c032eef4eb08e80336a4fd295458016b467bea7b5c`;
  no PPM was written because no post-phase-6 sample was nonblack.
- Existing owner-level service: the GXRuntime dependency already promotes
  retail GX display-copy to the Aurora frame boundary. Do not reimplement or
  bypass it.
- Authentic renderer differential: a 31-frame `.dolt` trace spanning presents
  590-620 replays 2,307 plans with no skips/rejects through both GXCore and the
  independent live Aurora path; both outputs are black. This falsifies a
  GXCore-only failure in that window. Trace SHA-256:
  `3c01bdcdf792652b8d71d4b3976ebb1f566bb9770d7f2f1ee1aa695581370d54`.
- Source timing: `dScnOpen_proc_c::proc_execute` waits 90 scene ticks before
  starting the first message, and `create` sets the scene to 30 Hz. The 1050
  bound ends only 103 retraces after phase 6, short of the approximately
  180-retrace source timer. The observed black window is not yet a stall.
- Resolution: the same continuous observer records its first nonblack frame at
  retrace 1083, 199 retraces after `Opening.arc` construction and consistent
  with the source-defined 90 ticks at 30 Hz plus transition overhead. The frame
  contains 37,475 nonblack pixels with hash `0xB9F56B30B21E2CAE`; visual
  inspection shows the original narration, "This is but one of the legends of
  which the people speak..." The authentic run stops normally at retrace 1200
  with 211/211 JAS forwards, zero ARAM rejects, authentic donor DMA, and
  148,604 GX submissions with zero rejects/failures.
- Evidence: ignored log
  `local-research/evidence/reorientation-20260825/bluewake-opening-donor-source-timed-1200.log`,
  SHA-256 `d9b5f95958eb27913731c6c3c69d7a29a77fd3deb172c84640b935ab21119764`;
  captured PPM SHA-256
  `4471ddcdb6478d09b1876e66609fa77ebdfcea7f0cf8954a9aa7c723ae7eccfb`.
- Prohibited: a second emulator, fixed-retrace input, direct guest-state writes,
  or treating a headless milestone as proof of visible presentation.
- No simulator used.

### BW-P4-0055 - Donor-backed JAS boot-resource lifecycle stalls before title
- Category: CPU_RUNTIME; state: resolved; priority: S1; owner: donor DSP
  interrupt delivery and JAS/DVD scheduler contract.
- Root cause: a stale host intervention forced MSR.EE on at selector
  continuation `0x80307EAC`. Retail `SelectThread` still owned an
  interrupt-masked scheduler critical section there, so donor-frequency
  external interrupts entered JAS and destroyed current-thread/run-queue
  ownership. A hard-coded audio no-preempt PC rewrite was also removed after
  donor/control tests proved it non-causal.
- Differential proof: before removal, donor and adapter-inactive routes match
  through retrace 5, then the donor route alone collapses at retrace 6 with 48
  queued messages, null current thread, idle context, and zero run bits. Six
  external interrupts land in JAS context across that interval. With the
  forced-EE intervention removed, donor and control both retain JAS ownership
  through retrace 6 and complete LOD with an empty queue by retrace 7.
- Authentic resolution: the donor-backed run reaches title at retrace 330 and
  stops normally at retrace 360. It records 209/209 JAS forwards,
  `active_done=1`, an empty queue, 4,295 ARAM transfers with zero rejects or
  pending interrupt, authentic note events, and first nonzero donor DMA at
  retrace 351.
- Evidence: ignored log
  `local-research/evidence/reorientation-20260825/bluewake-donor-title-no-idle-restore-360.log`,
  SHA-256 `f672a8abe4cb419b983491ef03afbaebcadbdbcc2c4c25faaf88e0b2a04cf35d`.
- Regression status: adapter and non-adapter hosts build; 16/16 runnable CTests
  and CPU ABI contracts pass. The donor-backed no-disc 100,000-block control
  stops normally at `0x80022910`, SHA-256
  `c450f04f90949d04275fa0db72e80abdbb55455664624d607ba945be1843fa2e`.
- Prohibited follow-up: do not restore either address-specific scheduler hook,
  trace another heap/ARAM boundary, or treat adapter-inactive results as
  authentic evidence.

### BW-P4-0053 - Event-synchronized title input and file-select transition
- Category: INPUT; state: resolved; priority: S1; owner: macOS SI/PAD harness
  and original title/Name-scene route.
- Root cause: the old SI status payload mixed controller type `0x09` into the
  logical SPEC2 button word, holding A+Y at neutral and preventing a clean
  edge. After title confirmation, the no-card retail route also intentionally
  waits for message dismissal and a “continue without saving” selection.
- Implemented result: the promoted encoder emits `0x00008080` for neutral and
  `0x01008080` for A. One-shot button/axis events are armed only by title-ready,
  the title actor's `fopOvlpM_IsPeek()==0` return, retail error-message wait,
  and retail yes/no state. Inputs still flow through SI, translated PAD, and
  CPad trigger generation; no guest state or scene is forced.
- Resolution: the authentic route reaches title-ready at retrace 330, accepts
  title confirmation at 386, creates the Name scene at 448, begins its retail
  memory-card path at 456, dismisses the no-card message at 523, selects and
  confirms “continue without saving” at 577/583, and enters `FileSelectOpen`
  at retrace 646. It stops normally at retrace 700 after 39,450,524 blocks;
  210/210 JAS forwards and 4,310 ARAM transfers complete with zero
  failures/rejects/pending interrupt. P4 milestone 4 passes.
- Regression tests: `bluewake_pad_event_schedule_test`,
  `bluewake_pad_wire_test`, 16/16 runnable CTests, CPU ABI contracts, and the
  100,000-block no-disc smoke pass.

### BW-P4-0052 - JAS DVD async lifecycle does not complete the boot-resource batch
- Category: CPU_RUNTIME; state: resolved; priority: S1; owner: JAS/DVD command
  execution and asynchronous completion publication.
- First seen: same-disc reference comparison on 2026-08-25.
- Gate/content: P4 authentic macOS boot through title readiness.
- Stable signature: corrected-clock BlueWake stops normally at 500 retraces
  with `title_ready=0`. Dolphin first enters the title fade at dumped frame
  475 and has a full title sky by 483. At 600 BlueWake retraces, 24,481 donor
  DSP DMA callbacks remain zero and no `BankMgr::noteOn` occurs; Dolphin's
  first nonzero DSP sample is at approximately retrace 573.
- Measured ownership: retail `dScnLogo::dvdWaitDraw` requests the opening scene
  only after `dComIfG_syncAllObjectRes` and every named boot archive/data
  command reports synchronized. The BlueWake title REL entry
  `0x81E01B88` is not executed by retrace 500.
- Falsified hypothesis: the one-shot `dvdWaitDraw` snapshot did not identify a
  first false operand. At retrace 475, `dComIfG_syncAllObjectRes` returns `1`
  and all 30 named command objects have `mIsDone=0`; the entire common batch is
  pending.
- Measured upstream path: `mDoDvdThd` receives and cuts commands. Because
  `SyncWidthSound=1`, it forwards each callback through
  `JASystem::Dvd::sThread` (`0x806AC760`), whose `OSThread` record is
  `0x806AD7E0` and message queue is `0x806AC790`. This common handoff is earlier
  than every individual archive completion.
- Current inference: the common JAS DVD task accepts and executes commands but
  its asynchronous command lifecycle does not drain the batch coherently.
  Missing title, notes, and PCM are downstream.
- Tested subhypothesis: the audio-address-specific external-interrupt context
  preservation was generalized into a default-on context-coherence subsystem
  and executable test. The authentic 476-retrace output remained byte-identical
  to the prior control and every command remained incomplete, so context-save
  materialization alone is not causal for this blocker.
- Falsified subhypothesis: a composite-backed test executes the translated
  `OSReceiveMessage` empty-queue block and translated `OSSendMessage` wake,
  including `SelectThread`, `OSLoadContext`, coroutine resume, and message
  consumption. The arbitrary receiver is resumed coherently, so generic OS
  message scheduling is not the active owner.
- Localized lifecycle: a ten-retrace authentic donor run records 35 accepted
  JAS forwards and zero failures. Command `0x80ADEFDC`, retained as
  `l_lodCommand` for `/res/Stage/sea/LODALL.arc`, owns the in-flight callback;
  48 JAS messages are queued behind it. This is command execution/completion,
  not generic message delivery.
- Reorientation §5 result: one corrected-clock re-observation sees coherent
  8,192-byte requests from staging source `0x80ADBF00` and destinations
  advancing by `0x2000`. The old malformed allocator request is not reproduced,
  and the ARAM/heap provenance route remains closed.
- Falsified subhypothesis: a bounded composite-backed execution of translated
  `JKRAramStream::writeToAram` exhausts `0x5000` as three coherent DMA calls,
  returns the full byte count, and publishes completion through translated
  `OSSendMessage`. Translator loop state and synchronous stream completion are
  not the active owner.
- Existing authentic evidence shows the earlier `0xE4A40` stream completes
  before the JAS-owned `0x78EE0` stream starts. The host currently performs
  ARAM copies/completion by recognizing translated `ARQPostRequest` and ISR PCs,
  with one path gated to thread `0x804211E0`; this is not a promoted hardware
  service. The guest already writes the retail DSP/ARAM DMA register tuple.
- Falsifiable hypothesis: a default-on register-level ARAM DMA subsystem that
  reconstructs each aligned transfer from the six guest-programmed registers,
  commits the copy once, raises one DSP interrupt, and clears it on guest
  acknowledgement will let translated ARQ own chunking/callback progression
  without PC or thread identity hooks.
- Implemented result: the promoted register device passes both-direction,
  acknowledgement, pending-rejection, and ARAM-mirroring tests. The PC/thread
  interception is removed. A 10-retrace authentic donor replay performs 2,293
  transfers with zero rejects/pending completion; `l_lodCommand` changes to
  done, the JAS queue drains from 48 to zero, and its thread sleeps normally.
  This confirms the hypothesis and repairs the localized command stall.
- Reference-boundary result: retrace 476 remains title-late, but the device is
  coherent (3,121 transfers, zero rejects/pending) and `l_lodCommand` is done.
  The new earliest invariant is five messages in the JAS task's input
  `JKRThread::mMessageQueue` while its consumer is sleeping. Source finds no
  true-pause caller, so expected paused residue is ruled out.
- Reframed root cause: retained state proves the consumer is blocked on stream
  command `0x80ADDF10`'s private completion queue (`jas_wait=0x80ADDF44`), not
  its five-message input queue. The host excluded translated callback
  `0x802BCC78` (`JKRDvdFile::doneProcess`) and directly enqueued only when the
  receiver list was empty. It therefore skipped the authentic blocked receiver
  instead of executing translated `OSSendMessage` and waking it.
- Implemented result: a promoted hardware-callback service masks EE, brackets
  translated callback execution with the guest scheduler lock used by the
  retail external-interrupt dispatcher, preserves the interrupted register
  image, and retains callback memory effects. The callback exclusion and
  address-specific direct queue write are removed. The composite-backed test
  proves a blocked receiver moves to its priority run queue without switching
  inside the callback.
- Authentic control: a 4.1M donor replay stops normally at retrace 22, reaches
  logo rendering, accepts 35/35 JAS forwards, reports the active command done
  and JAS input queue empty, and completes 3,953 ARAM transfers with zero
  rejects/pending interrupt. This closes the localized completion stall but
  does not yet qualify the retrace-476 title frontier.
- Resolution: the post-fix reference control reaches title-ready at retrace 330
  and stops normally at retrace 476 after 3,855,600,198 cycles. It records
  209/209 JAS forwards, an empty JAS queue, the active command done, and 4,295
  ARAM transfers with zero rejects/pending interrupt. P4 milestone 3 passes;
  continue under `BW-P4-0053`.
- Evidence: `docs/archive/status/REFERENCE_ORACLE_2026-08-25.md`; private ignored logs
  SHA-256 `29cc8ae3ec335f5570f6701dea4d4a818aba126a5abe2186d4e685b0430b26ee`

  `c44c028ed281585004c58043bba8c951f162ec5172bcfd5b116125c6ac50e11c`
  (600 retraces); one-shot resource snapshot

### BW-P4-0050 - Promoted guest-clock ownership of VI and AI cadence
- Category: CPU_RUNTIME; state: resolved; priority: S1; owner: host main loop.
- First seen: accepted independent review dated 2026-08-25.
- Gate/content: P4 authentic macOS boot, pacing, title readiness, input timing,
  and audio qualification.
- Former stable signature: `runtime/host/src/main.c` advanced timebase, decrementer,
  and donor DSP from generated cycle charges, but asserts VI retrace and PAD
  polling every 1,000,000 dispatched blocks. `dol_audio_dma_set_vi_clock`
  derives AI work cadence from that same block count. The clean 20M route
  presents only 16 frames while guest-time-driven splash transitions continue.
- Tier-0 measurement: the old first three retraces occurred at timebase
  1,844,032, 22,409,381, and 43,743,143. Steady deltas were 20,565,349 and
  21,333,762 ticks, 30.47x and 31.61x the 675,000 target.
- Implemented correction: `DolViClock` advances from generated CPU cycles at
  8,100,000 cycles/retrace; PAD polling follows each due VI event; audio DMA
  advances by the same variable cycle charge at 486 MHz. The block counter no
  longer drives a guest device.
- Falsifiable hypothesis: if Tier-0 measurement shows substantially more than
  675,000 timebase ticks per retrace, then deriving VI and AI cadence from the
  promoted guest clock will restore hardware-proportional pacing and materially
  deepen boot progress per dispatched-block bound. A near-675,000 measurement
  falsifies this blocker framing.
- Verification result: the corrected 3M authentic headless control stops
  normally at `0x80307EF4`; its first 16 retrace intervals are
  674,986-675,016 ticks. Focused compiled suites pass. Nested runtime commit:
  `1f08d14ec5`.
- Tier-2/Tier-3 result: no-disc smoke stops normally. The 20M native and
  headless routes both stop normally; the final headless clock summary records
  399 retraces over 269,405,654 timebase ticks (399.12 nominal periods) with
  `vi_pending=0`.
- Resolution: close the two-clock implementation defect. Continue under
  `BW-P4-0051`; do not reuse stale fixed-retrace milestones.
- Prohibited until resolved: fixed-retrace input schedules, larger unchanged
  boot bounds, zero-PCM/track tracing, and another address-specific scheduler

- Evidence: `docs/archive/status/INDEPENDENT_REVIEW_2026-08-25.md`;

  `714281279d9e195c095e167b45494a1e3f0afe0f91cf52fb2424dd77cbf73a98`);

### BW-P4-0049 - Zero PCM not reproduced as an independent defect
- Category: AUDIO; state: blocked-on `BW-P4-0053`; priority: S1.
- Reframing: the donor task executes and DMAs coherent silent mixer frames.
  BlueWake now reaches the title scene at retrace 330. Dolphin's first nonzero
  output around retrace 573 and BlueWake's old all-zero retrace-600 control are
  no longer route-comparable because the callback fix changed progression.
  Re-observe only after `BW-P4-0053` qualifies title input/file select and the
  promoted route reaches the reference note-event boundary.
- Historical record follows; its earlier `active` wording is superseded.
- Original category: CPU_RUNTIME / AUDIO; original priority: S1.
- Reproducer: explicit DSP build with the disc-backed headless route,
  `BLUEWAKE_MAX_BLOCKS=600000`, `BLUEWAKE_TRACE_AUDIO_OBJECT=1`,
  `BLUEWAKE_TRACE_DSP_LLE=1`, and the pinned donor IROM/COEF inputs.
- Evidence: `/tmp/bluewake-goal-loop-command-payload-600k-20260825.log`;
  `/tmp/bluewake-goal-loop-task-payload-600k-20260825.log`.
- Stable signature (historical, resolved subproblem): the authentic callback reaches `0x80289024` with command
  base `0x803EA4B0` and coherent command words, then the donor receives exactly
  the two CPU mailbox words `0x8D00000A` and `0x803EA5E0`. No `0x82xx` mixer
  words or hidden third mailbox word precede the donor's DSP PC `0x002D`
  write of `DSBL=0x8046`, external address `0`, DSP address `0x0300`, and
  control `0`; the donor previously interpreted the start bit as an oversized
  length and exited status 0 before host normal stop. The
  task descriptor itself is coherent: IRAM `0x00399420/0x1D20`, DRAM
  `0x003EAFC0/0x2000`, vectors `0/0x10`.
- Current inference: the donor exit was a source-compatibility mismatch at the
  DSBL register boundary, not DSP ROM state, task-descriptor corruption, a
  missing mixer payload, host sink, allocator, or ARAM. The adapter now strips
  only hardware start bit 15 before forwarding the authentic length; this is
  covered by the adapter regression.
- Ruled out: malformed active `DSPTaskInfo`; host-side task pointer/length
  corruption; donor initialization; mailbox boot sequence; downstream heap or
  ARAM boundary trace.
- Next action: the later mailbox-busy wait at `0x8028EDFC` is resolved by the
  donor scheduler/control increment below. Change experiment shape and run the
  native macOS Aurora presentation route with the promoted DSP subsystem;
  measure visible boot/progression and nonzero audio content before opening a
  narrower remaining blocker.
- Regression: focused `bluewake_dsp_adapter_test`, C ABI test, and
  `gxruntime_tests` pass 3/3 after rebuild.
- Evidence command: serialized single-host macOS replay; no simulator used.
- New evidence: `/tmp/bluewake-goal-loop-queue-cursor-600k-20260825.log`.
- Analyzer-fix evidence: `/tmp/bluewake-goal-loop-analyzer-fix-600k-20260825.log`.

#### Resolved increment - donor DSP scheduler and live control ownership (2026-08-25)
- BlueWake now follows the donor LLE contract: 12,600 CPU-cycle update cadence,
  72 CPU-cycle mailbox synchronization, and a 6:1 CPU/DSP cycle ratio.
- The adapter retries the hardware-visible `CR_EXTERNAL_INT` request until the
  DSP interrupt mask accepts it. The host composes live donor control bits with
  `DSP_CONTROL_MASK=0x0C07`, eliminating the stale external-interrupt bit that
  woke the halted task before the guest sent `CDD10001`.
- The authentic 800,000-block replay clears `0x80000002`, completes
  `DCD10002`/`CDD10001`, repeats `DCD10004`, advances audio DMA, loads retail
  audio banks, and stops normally. The clean 3,000,000-block replay reaches
  later `DCD10005`/`CDD10003` transitions and stops normally at
  `pc=0x80307EF4`; the old `0x8028EDFC` wait does not recur.
- Verification: focused adapter/C ABI/GXRuntime tests pass 3/3; the CPU/runtime
  contract suite passes 11/11. No simulator was used.
- Evidence: `/tmp/bluewake-dsp-control-mask-800k-20260825.log`;
  `/tmp/bluewake-dsp-control-mask-3m-20260825.log` (SHA-256
  `afb241d97ec937997ea60e45abb188346df0d16e893e0cd7de411bcc0d734086`).

  The four-million-block window capture is still black, so visible boot and

  `c4313d82ee5b582657a087a8beb33944d305db371bd2be38c506e11d8fc4c7bd`);

#### Resolved increment - DSP accelerator ARAM ownership (2026-08-25)
- Dolphin's donor `ReadHostMemory`/`WriteHostMemory` contract accesses ARAM,
  while `DMAToDSP`/`DMAFromDSP` accesses CPU memory. BlueWake previously sent
  both through MEM1 callbacks, allowing loaded `jdsp` initialization writes to
  overwrite low-memory OS state, including `OS_CURRENT_CONTEXT`.
- The adapter C++ and C APIs now carry distinct ARAM callbacks backed by the
  shared GXRuntime ARAM model. A focused task-boot sentinel proves MEM1 is not
  modified by accelerator writes; all three focused tests pass.
- Authentic replay now reaches `__DSPHandler`, consumes `DCD10000`, sends the
  first task queue, and executes request callbacks. This closes the boot
  interrupt blocker without injecting a mailbox word or changing scheduler
  state.

#### Resolved increment - DSBL start-flag compatibility (2026-08-25)
- Generated guest code at `0x803169B4..0x803169C4` explicitly ORs `0x8000`
  into `DSBL`; the donor consumes the low 15-bit byte count. The adapter now
  forwards `0x0046` while retaining the authentic write and start semantics.
- The focused adapter regression, C ABI test, and GXRuntime tests pass 3/3.
- The serialized 3,000,000-block replay reaches normal stop at
  `pc=0x8031907C` and records 162 authentic audio-DMA chunks, including ring
  wrap. No donor `DoDMA()` exit or explicit nonzero PCM marker appears.
- Evidence: `/tmp/bluewake-goal-loop-dma-start-3m-20260825.log`;
  `runtime/host/src/dsp_adapter.cpp`; `runtime/host/src/dsp_adapter_test.cpp`.

#### Resolved increment - initial AI/DAC frame scheduling (2026-08-25)
- The authentic DSP-source interrupt now dispatches `__AIDHandler` at
  `0x80316F0C`, reaches `syncAudio()`/the audio queue, and runs the message-0
  `Kernel::updateDac()` continuation. `finishDSPFrame()` is observed at
  `0x8028EFC0`, with `snIntCount=7`, and submits the authentic
  `0x82074000`, `0x806AFFA0`, and `0x806B0400` mailbox sequence.
- The donor receives that sequence and remains stable. The corrected trace
  shows loaded IRAM task code at `0x06C5`/`0x06C9`; its coherent `WR0` state
  and count read still leave the consumer at the high half of a prior word,
  with no `DSCR/DSBL` write or DSP-to-CPU DMA.
- Remaining owner: donor DSP mailbox queue framing after `DsyncFrame2`; do not
  synthesize a frame command, write PCM directly, or reopen heap/ARAM tracing.
- Evidence: `/tmp/bluewake-goal-loop-dsp-status-3m-20260825.log`;
  `/tmp/bluewake-goal-loop-frame-trace-600k-20260825.log`;

### BW-P4-0049 continuation - audio thread exits at DSP-status boundary
- Category: CPU_RUNTIME / AUDIO; state: active; priority: S1.
- Reproducer: explicit DSP build with the disc-backed headless route,
  `BLUEWAKE_MAX_BLOCKS=600000`, `BLUEWAKE_TRACE_AUDIO_OBJECT=1`, and the
  pinned donor IROM/COEF inputs.
- Evidence: `/tmp/bluewake-goal-loop-control-600k-20260825.log` and
  `/tmp/bluewake-goal-loop-lldb-20260825.log`.
- Stable signature: authentic DSP task callbacks complete; the translated
  audio thread reaches `0x80289038`, calls retail helper `0x803169B4`, and
  the process exits with status 0 before the host prints its normal-stop line.
  No `0x8200` mixer command or sustained AI-DMA/PCM evidence is present in
  this bounded route.
- Current inference: the unresolved boundary is the guest
  `OSReceiveMessage`/sleep continuation and host control return, not donor
  mailbox format, allocator/ARAM state, or the platform sink. The debugger
  shows an exit rather than a native crash.

The new authentic `startSoundActor` observation proves the source-native frame scheduler reaches JAudio sequence dispatch. ### Historical: BW-P4-0041 pre-correction signature

### Resolved module-60 lifecycle boundary: BW-P4-0027
- Category: CPU_RUNTIME / REL_LIFECYCLE / TRANSLATOR
- First seen: 2026-08-23, after the full per-module-base composite regeneration
- Gate/content: P4 authentic boot; title/room setup advances, normal gameplay not reached
- Reproducer: `BLUEWAKE_DOL=generated/full/main.dol BLUEWAKE_DISC='ref/The Legend Of Zelda The Wind Waker.iso' BLUEWAKE_RELS_DIR=generated/full/rels BLUEWAKE_PAD_BUTTONS=0x0100 BLUEWAKE_PAD_PULSE_RETRACE=96 BLUEWAKE_PAD_PULSE_LENGTH=2 BLUEWAKE_MAX_BLOCKS=17000000 ./build/runtime-host/bluewake_host /tmp/bluewake-current-build-20260823/gGZLE01_recomp.dylib`
- Normalized signature: authentic `d_a_obj_ikada` prolog enters raw `0x81E19654` through linked `0x815500F4`; SDA restore reports `r13=0x803FE0E0`; execution then reaches unmapped `pc=0x80000000` with `lr=0x815501C0` after 16,132,314 blocks.
- Measured facts: the old d_a_ship `0xDEE00000` fallback is absent from the fresh composite; the route loads d_a_ship and later materializes d_a_obj_ikada. The replay contains no Program exception, ARAM corruption signature, allocator failure, or fabricated-clock symptom before the stop.
- Hypothesis: the remaining boundary is the authentic composite guest-layout contract at module-60 REL lifecycle continuation. The canonical batch output preserves the raw relocation contract, and the generated `bctrl`/`blr` path preserves LR/CTR through `0x815501C0` and `0x81550348`. The zero target follows because the retail heap allocates an object at `0x81558160` while fixed module-60 BSS begins at `0x815581A0`; the object's `+64` field therefore aliases the BSS chain head. This is a fixed-linked-image reservation problem, not a raw symbol/return ABI defect.
- Regression tests: canonical folder batch (`543/543` REL chunks); composite provenance audit (`753/753` files); one serialized authentic A-pulse replay; prior runtime and translation suites remain passing.
- Evidence: `local-research/evidence/p4-authentic-current-rel-fixed-17m-20260823.log`; `local-research/evidence/p4-authentic-canonical-rel-batch-r8-17m-20260823.log`; `local-research/evidence/p4-authentic-rel-fixed-jkr-actual-r3-r7-17m-20260823.log`; `/tmp/bluewake-rel-batch-probe-20260823.log`; `/tmp/bluewake-current-build-20260823/gGZLE01_recomp.dylib`.
- State/priority: resolved / S1 -> BW-P4-0028
- Resolution: the owner was a `JKRSolidHeap` spanning `[0x815306F0,0x817FEC50)`, not an ExpHeap free list. ### BW-P4-0009 - No authentic guest time base + decrementer delivery
- State/priority: resolved into BW-P4-0012 / S2; the promoted clock is corrected and no longer active.
- Category: CPU_RUNTIME
- First seen: 2026-08-22, external reorientation and clock promotion
- Gate/content: P4 authentic boot; display alarm, second logo draw, and title/opening request
- Reproducer: tiered public clock test, composite smoke, then `BLUEWAKE_DOL=generated/full/main.dol BLUEWAKE_DISC=<private GZLE01-disc-image> BLUEWAKE_RELS_DIR=generated/full/rels BLUEWAKE_TRACE_RUNQUEUE=1 BLUEWAKE_TRACE_DECREMENTER=1 BLUEWAKE_MAX_BLOCKS=8000000 ./build/runtime-host/bluewake_host build/composite-lib/gGZLE01_recomp.dylib`
- Normalized signature before promotion: guest display alarm deadline is queued, but no authentic time-base/decrementer service owns its delivery; downstream ARAM corruption appeared only with rejected/opt-in timing workarounds.
- Hypothesis: one monotonic elapsed guest clock shared by `CPUState.timebase`, SPR-22, and `__OSGetSystemTime` will cross the alarm deadline and deliver the translated retail decrementer callback, producing the second logo draw while the no-disc control remains unchanged.
- Implementation: promoted default-on `guest_clock_advance` in `runtime/host/src/main.c`; no environment gate or ARAM/allocator change.
- Regression test: `python3 tests/test_guest_clock.py`; then `python3 tests/test_alarm_deadline.py`, runtime-host build, and `python3 tests/test_composite.py`.
- Evidence: `tests/test_guest_clock.py`; `docs/archive/status/REORIENTATION_2026-08-22.md` §4. Authentic replay is pending this tiered verification.
- Ruled out for the active boundary: allocator defect, ARAM request construction, and downstream heap ownership. Those were superseded by decision until an authentic post-clock replay re-observes them.
- State/priority: superseded by BW-P4-0011 / S2; the corrected clock now defines the clean title-progression signature.
- Verification: focused unit, alarm/allocator regressions, composite (`12/12`), and runtime-host build pass. One authentic default-on replay loaded 2391 FST entries, delivered repeated decrementer exceptions, reached multiple logo draws and scene requests `request_id=1`/`request_id=2`, then stopped at the malformed ARAM request after 7,763,161 blocks.
- Evidence: `local-research/evidence/p4-guest-clock-promoted-authentic-8m-20260822.log`.
- Result: the initial implementation exposed a unit error: Gekko cycle charges were being treated as timebase ticks. Correcting the 12:1 ratio and carrying fractional cycles removes the malformed ARAM terminal on the authentic route. The heap/ARAM corruption hunt is superseded by decision.
- Next action: compare the clean scheduler/title presentation handoff after the corrected clock; do not trace heap/ARAM ownership.

### BW-P4-0008 - Default-on coherent clock reaches retrace four, then PPCHalt
- State/priority: superseded by BW-P4-0012 / S2; its pre-ratio interpretation is historical control evidence, not the active authentic blocker.
- Category: CPU_RUNTIME / OS_SERVICE / GRAPHICS
- First seen: 2026-08-22, default-clock promotion
- Gate/content: P4 authentic boot; no title/gameplay milestone promoted
- Reproducer: `BLUEWAKE_DOL=generated/full/main.dol BLUEWAKE_DISC=<private GZLE01-disc-image> BLUEWAKE_RELS_DIR=generated/full/rels BLUEWAKE_TRACE_RUNQUEUE=1 BLUEWAKE_TRACE_DECREMENTER=1 BLUEWAKE_MAX_BLOCKS=8000000 ./build/runtime-host/bluewake_host build/composite-lib/gGZLE01_recomp.dylib`
- Normalized signature: repeated default-on decrementer delivery reaches the fourth VI retrace and scene/frame callbacks, then enters `PPCHalt` at `0x8030150C` around 5,000,000 blocks; bounded run ends at `0x80301510`.
- Measured facts: the legacy composite has no per-dispatch cycle charges, so the host's deterministic elapsed charge advances `CPUState.timebase` and SPR-22 from one source; default-on delivery enters the translated retail decrementer handler; the route reaches `JUTVideo::postRetraceProc #4`, wakes main, and emits scene/frame callbacks before the halt. No title/gameplay milestone or ARAM overlap is observed.
- Inference: promoting the clock removed the prior no-decrementer idle plateau, but the first default route exposes a context/return or scheduler interaction before the later title path. This is now the earliest blocker; the prior malformed-ARAM trace is downstream and remains unclassified.
- Comparison: the historical elapsed opt-in route later delivered a decrementer on worker context `0x804211E0` interrupted at `0x802B45AC` and reached the ARAM request; the default route's final expiry remains on the main path at `0x80307EF4`/`0x802C78A8` before `PPCHalt`.
- Context result: a bounded six-million-block trace observed 22 default expiries with `current_context == 0x803F09E8`, saved PC `0x80307EF4`, and no runnable thread; retrace wake made main ready before the route returned to idle. This rules out a stale context pointer and shifts the next boundary to scheduler work production after the timer-driven wake.
- Alarm/stream result: the default route's queue head is periodic alarm `0x803A8640` with handler `0x80018D44` and deadline `48,341,935`; the display alarm is never reached. The ARAM worker initializes and waits, but no later ARAM manager send is published before the idle plateau.
- Ruled out for this iteration: missing SPR-22 instruction plumbing, absent decrementer vector entry, absent first expiry, and a host-only heap write as the immediate blocker.
- Regression tests: `cmake --build build/runtime-host -j2`; `python3 tests/test_alarm_deadline.py`; `python3 tests/test_aram_allocator.py`; `python3 tests/test_composite.py` (`12/12 PASS`).
- Evidence: `local-research/evidence/p4-default-clock-authentic-8m-20260822.log`.
- State/priority: superseded by BW-P4-0011 / S2; the corrected clock replaces this pre-ratio signature.
- Next action: compare the first missing ARAM stream/manager publication after worker initialization against the historical worker-context route; do not alter ARAM request handling or force a resume.

### BW-P4-0006 - Main thread has no runnable scheduler path after logo setup
- Category: OS_SERVICE / CPU_RUNTIME / GRAPHICS
- First seen: 2026-08-21, P4 host bring-up
- Gate/content: P4 entry through low-level initialization; no milestone promoted
- Reproducer: `BLUEWAKE_DOL=generated/full/main.dol BLUEWAKE_DISC=<private GZLE01-disc-image> BLUEWAKE_RELS_DIR=generated/full/rels BLUEWAKE_MAX_BLOCKS=5000000 ./build/runtime-host/bluewake_host build/composite-lib/gGZLE01_recomp.dylib`
- Normalized signature: `normal after 10000000 blocks pc=0x80304DF8`; after the fourth VI retrace, the scheduler alternates between `0x80307EAC` and `0x80304DF8` without a title transition.
- Measured facts: the guest clears VI status `0x9107 -> 0x1107`; the guarded external-delivery path preserves the saved main context and `OSLoadContext` returns to `0x80307EAC` with `srr1=0x00001002`; `JUTVideo::postRetraceProc #4` temporarily sets `OSCurrentContext` to `0x8040CB28` and restores `0x803A2960`; the main video consumer receives the retrace message again after the guard is narrowed; `title_dolby_mark.bti` and additional DVD/JAS queue receives are reached; a runnable worker context at `0x804211E0` remains at selector `0x80307EAC` with `MSR=0x1032` while the 10M route alternates through the dispatcher. A bounded worker-queue probe found no late `OSReceiveMessage` entry on `0x8039CD60`, so there is no measured stale receive-queue transition to repair yet.
- Inference: the prior main-thread consumer stall was caused by over-broad interrupt re-enabling at the selector and is corrected. The remaining failure is narrower: at `0x802B5FE0` (`JKRAramPiece::doneDMA`), the promoted ARAM worker repeatedly sees low ARQ request `0x80ADBE40` and callback `0x802B5FE0` still linked, with a zero post-service request length, while manager queue `0x8039CD60` is empty and the worker remains runnable at priority 6. The same callback continuation repeats with advancing destinations. Stream sync and manager payload traces show valid advancing transfers, and the post-store trace proves selected-thread installation and current-context selection are correct. The selector-condition trace also shows the restored `r3=1` and CR take the expected retail branches. The unresolved boundary is now the owner-specific DVD/ARAM message wake chain after deferred callback enqueue, not restored context state, priority selection, callback ordering, or current-thread installation.
- Regression test: `python3 tests/test_composite.py` remains 12/12; the focused host reproducer and `BLUEWAKE_MAX_BLOCKS` bound provide the current scheduler smoke route, but no synthetic scheduler oracle has been added yet.
- Ruled out: repeated DVD callback suppression, missing ARAM chunk completion, missing DSP interrupt delivery, missing archive entries, VI status acknowledgement failure, and overwriting the main context with the dispatcher PC.
- Failed experiment: restoring the static `IdleContext` and clearing the current-thread pointer at the empty selector regressed to `PPCHalt` at `0x80301510`; the experiment was removed.
- Failed experiment: suppressing external delivery across the worker selector range `0x80307EAC..0x80307FB4` parked the route at `0x80307EAC` from 4M through 10M blocks; the guard extension was removed.
- Failed experiment: forcing the restored worker selector return value from `r3=1` to `r3=0` did not advance the title route; the bounded run regressed to `0x80307EF4` with the main queue cleared by retrace four, so the override was removed.
- Failed experiment: opt-in ARAM interrupt latency of two dispatches preserved the title lookup but still left the worker cycling; latency of 100 dispatches regressed to `0x80307EF4` before the title path, so immediate completion timing is not the primary cause and the hook was removed.
- Failed experiment: suppressing the host follow-up when the observed ARQ request length was zero regressed before title setup to `0x80307EF4`; the observation occurs at a translated dispatch boundary and cannot be treated as the final request-unlink point.
- Failed experiment: deferring the host follow-up until the later cleared boundary prevented the callback continuation and regressed to `0x80307EF4`; the follow-up currently drives the translated callback path and cannot be reordered independently.
- Failed experiment: executing `JKRDvdFile::doneProcess` through the nested guest dispatcher entered scheduler code and raised a program exception at `0x00000C00`; the opt-in path was removed and the normal host-side callback model remains unchanged.
- Evidence: `local-research/evidence/p4-osloadcontext-atomic-20260821.log` records the guarded return and the original state-2/no-run-queue plateau. - Normalized signature: authentic route reaches five retraces, multiple draw/management passes, a logo draw callback, and a later scene request, then stops normally at `0x80307EF4` before title/gameplay.
- Measured facts: `OSWakeupThread` continuation at `0x80308BA4` clears GX `FinishQueue` (`0x803F7C9C`), sets main state `READY`, assigns run queue `0x803F0338`, and raises `RunQueueBits=0x00008000`; the selector later installs the main context. The route records `draw-iter` passes, logo tag `0x80ACDD44`, `retraces=5`, and a subsequent scene request before the bounded stop.
- Inference: BW-P4-0006's scheduler sub-boundary is resolved. The selector
  trace shows `SelectThread` selecting main `0x803A2960` at `0x80307FA0`,
  installing its context, clearing the priority-16 run bit, and resuming main
  at `0x80307EAC` in `RUNNING` state. The remaining earliest blocker is the
  handoff from the completed logo request through scene/title process creation
  and the next resource/frame milestone.
- Ruled out for this boundary: missing PE finish source delivery, missing `GXFinishInterruptHandler`, missing `DrawDone`, malformed `FinishQueue` insertion/removal, and a permanently unrunnable main thread.

  ends normally at `0x80307EF4` after 3,000,000 blocks; no authentic selector

- Hypothesis result: the direct host interrupt path must preserve the
  exception-state bit set by `OSExceptionVector`; without it, translated
  `OSLoadContext` takes the non-exception GPR restore branch and does not
  restore `r5-r12`. The host now preserves context state and sets
  `OS_CONTEXT_STATE_EXC | FPSAVED` (`0x0003`). The no-disc control remains
  normal after 3M blocks, and the authentic route reaches the title-Dolby and
  later resource milestones before the corrected idle signature.
- Source verification: pinned `OSContext.h` defines the two state bits, and
  the generated `OSLoadContext` tests/clears `OS_CONTEXT_STATE_EXC` before
  restoring `r5-r12`; this is a source-backed runtime hypothesis, not a
  promoted P4 result.

  `OSSaveContext` when `context.state & OS_CONTEXT_STATE_EXC` is nonzero, so
  the missing bit could also explain overwriting the interrupted context at

Selector checkpoint `37b9fec` and ARAM checkpoint `d07879f` remain in history. Runtime-host build and `12/12` composite regression pass.

## Resolved
### BW-P4-0002 - Guest floating-point unavailable during authentic boot
- Category: CPU_RUNTIME
- Resolution: Added a logged architectural FP-unavailable transition that
  resumes at `srr0` with `MSR[FP]` enabled; DSP/ARAM model also exposes the
  observed ARAM mode and DMA status transitions.
- Verification: Default host run reaches `SelectThread` at `0x80307EF4` after
  passing the prior `0x802AE070` fault; no FP exception remains the terminal
  signature.

### BW-P4-0001 - Fresh composite artifact not linked after dispatcher fix
- Category: BUILD / TEST_HARNESS
- Resolution: Relinked the composite after correcting containing-chunk dispatch.
- Verification: ABI verifier passes; dylib SHA-256 is
  `915d02a68cc7cd3e4fc52d9abf8f6e132c2ce681438f67b2494a2726d9398035`; host
  no longer reports `unmapped pc=0x80003140` and reaches DSP initialization.

### BW-RARC-0001 - RARC parser returned 0 RELs from RELS.arc
- Category: INPUT
- Root cause: Incorrect RARC header layout assumption and missing Yaz0 decompression
- Resolution: Rewrote parser per standard RARC format; added yaz0_decompress();
  data-section base resolved by scanning for first Yaz0 magic after metadata
- Verification: 180/180 archived RELs found; all module IDs parse correctly

### BW-REL-0002 - parse_rel_header read num_sections from wrong offset
- Category: INPUT
- Root cause: Used offset 0x08 (prev field) instead of 0x0C (num_sections)
- Resolution: Corrected to 0x0C per standard REL format; added sanity bounds
- Verification: All 415 RELs produce valid module IDs and section counts

### BW-REL-0003 - DolRecomp rejects TWW RELs with alignment mismatch
- Category: TRANSLATOR / INPUT
- Root cause: TWW RELs declare section_alignment=8 but their exec sections are
  only 4-aligned. DolRecomp enforces the declared alignment strictly.
- Resolution: Patched the alignment field to 4 in extracted REL copies during
  prepare.py extraction (originals on disc remain untouched).
- Verification: All 415 RELs pass DolRecomp parsing without alignment errors.

### BW-RELOC-0004 - REL24 relocation target out of range at default base
- Category: RELOCATION
- Root cause: Default --rel-base 0x80500000 places REL code too far from DOL
  code, exceeding the signed 26-bit REL24 +/-32 MB displacement range.
- Resolution: Use --rel-base 0x80400000 for the REL batch translation.
- Verification: Zero relocation errors across all 415 modules with the new base.

## 2026-08-22 decrementer boundary diagnostic

- Experiment: installed an opt-in SPR-22 read/write observer under
  `BLUEWAKE_TRACE_DECREMENTER` and ran the full active composite for 8,000,000
  blocks with the private authentic input.
- Result: zero guest decrementer reads or writes; the route retained the normal
  stop at `0x80307EF4` and did not produce a heap-journal or invalid-ARAM
  regression.
- Inference: the missing timer event is before guest decrementer programming,
  at the host/OS alarm deadline-to-decrementer service boundary. The diagnostic
  callback does not deliver an exception or invoke an alarm callback.
- Evidence:
  `local-research/evidence/p4-decrementer-spr22-diagnostic-authentic-8m-20260822.log`.
- Next action: add a public synthetic alarm-deadline/underflow oracle from the
  retail contract, then use it to constrain any authentic timer-service patch.

The public oracle now lives in `scripts/alarm_deadline.py` with focused
coverage in `tests/test_alarm_deadline.py`. It passes on Python 3.8 and covers
u64 wraparound, queue-head eligibility, and periodic rearm arithmetic. It does
not implement or claim authentic alarm delivery.

## 2026-08-22 mtdec fallback correction

- Source fact: retail `PPCMtdec` is an `mtdec` instruction, decoded as SPR 22.
  The generated body at `0x803014FC` previously reached the generic fallback,
  whose XO-467 case advanced without updating the decrementer.
- Correction: `instruction_fallback` now routes SPR-22 `mtdec` and `mfdec`
  through the existing `PPCSPRRead`/`PPCSPRWrite` ABI. The host stores the
  counter but does not yet inject a decrementer exception.
- Authentic result: one full-active 8M route recorded writes `0x1823CEE0` and
  `0x0269F067` at `0x803014FC` and retained the normal stop at `0x80307EF4`.
- Evidence:
  `local-research/evidence/p4-decrementer-mtdec-fallback-authentic-8m-20260822.log`.
- Revised next action: add a separately gated counter decrement and exact
  exception-context entry, then compare the resulting retail callback state.

## 2026-08-22 gated decrementer expiry result

- Experiment: `BLUEWAKE_ENABLE_GUEST_DECREMENTER=1` decremented the corrected
  SPR-22 state and entered the translated retail decrementer prologue at
  `0x803023A4`, using context `0x803F09E8` and exception number 8.
- Result: the 50M-block authentic route delivered repeated callbacks and
  periodic rearm writes, reached 49 retraces and repeated real frame draws,
  then stopped normally at `0x80307EF4`. No invalid ARAM request or host
  exception occurred.
- Inference: exception-context entry and counter expiry are operational in the
  diagnostic route. The remaining blocker is callback/rearm state and the
  downstream title/resource transition, not the missing vector entry alone.
- Evidence:
  `local-research/evidence/p4-decrementer-expiry-context-optin-authentic-50m-20260822.log`.
- Next action: compare callback/rearm state against the historical timer route
  and isolate the first divergence before promoting any timer behavior.

## 2026-08-22 elapsed-charge timer refinement

- Experiment: the gated decrementer service now subtracts the same elapsed
  timebase charge that the host loop consumes from `cpu.downcount`.
- Result: the first expiry occurred at timebase `48,342,068`, matching the
  programmed `0x0269F067` deadline. The authentic route entered the translated
  decrementer handler, then stopped at the invalid ARAM request after
  `7,763,159` blocks at `0x802B5FCC`; no unmapped PC or host exception occurred.
- Inference: exact decrementer cadence is now aligned with the retail deadline.
  The remaining failure is earlier than title progression and is exposed after
  timer delivery, likely in callback/context rearm state or the resulting ARAM
  command sequence. The prior 49-retrace run used a coarse diagnostic cadence
  and is retained only as historical context, not as the correctness baseline.
- Evidence:
  `local-research/evidence/p4-decrementer-elapsed-context-optin-authentic-50m-20260822.log`.
- Next action: compare the first post-expiry context, alarm rearm, and ARAM
  command fields against the historical timer-assisted route.

## 2026-08-22 decrementer handler-entry correction

- Source fact: `DecrementerExceptionCallback` is at `0x803023A4`, while the
  retail assembly `DecrementerExceptionHandler` is at `0x803025D4`.
- Correction: the gated host now enters `0x803025D4`, allowing the translated
  handler prologue to save its registers/GQRs and establish the eight-byte
  stack linkage before branching to the callback.
- Result: the serialized 10M-block authentic route still stopped on the same
  invalid zero-length ARQ request at `0x802B5FCC`, after `7,763,161` blocks;
  there was no unmapped PC or host exception.
- Inference: the prior callback-entry mismatch was real but is not the sole
  cause of the downstream ARAM corruption. The remaining divergence is in the
  callback/rearm or ARAM-worker command state.
- Evidence:
  `local-research/evidence/p4-decrementer-handler-entry-authentic-10m-20260822.log`.
- Next action: compare the first corrupted ARAM command and heap fields with
  the pre-expiry control and identify the first writer.

## 2026-08-22 allocator control-field journal

- Instrumentation: narrowed the write journal to allocator control field
  `0x8041D104` and the tail block after broad heap initialization exhausted the
  diagnostic cap.
- Result: the final valid control pointer sequence reaches `0x80ACE444` at the
  translated allocator boundary `0x802B1DE0`/`0x802B1EC8`. The later
  `0x802B5C50` ARAM-piece call observes `mTailUsedList=0x1C17337B` and returns
  `0x1C17338B`, before the invalid zero-length message is published.
- Inference: the ARAM worker is a downstream consumer, not the first writer of
  this corruption. This corroborates the existing allocator boundary blocker
  and does not support another allocator-policy or queue-timing override.
- Evidence:
  `local-research/evidence/p4-decrementer-heap-head-field-writer-authentic-8m-20260822.log`.
- Next action: compare the final allocator register tuple at the
  `0x802B1DE0` return with the exact retail/generated write sequence.

## 2026-08-22 free-block-only journal

- Instrumentation: restricted the journal to the free-tail block at
  `0x80AD2140` so repeated used-list writes could not exhaust the trace.
- Result: after the fourth valid allocation, the failing `0x802B1DE0` dispatch
  observes `size=0x9B6A12D3` and `prev=0x86F11B9B` already present in the
  free-tail block. The dispatch republishes that malformed state, returns
  `0x1C17338B`, and the fifth `0x802B5C50` call publishes the zero-length ARAM
  message.
- Inference: the ARAM worker and final `appendUsedList` publication are
  downstream consumers. The first unresolved interval is between the fourth
  successful allocator return and the fifth allocator entry, including any

## 2026-08-22 retail ARQ contract comparison

- Source comparison: retail `JKRAramPiece::startDMA` forwards the command
  source/destination/direction/length to `ARQPostRequest`; retail
  `__ARQServiceQueueLo` uses a `0x1000` chunk and increments source and
  destination after each chunk.

  semantics and the expected ARAM-to-RAM direction. No host-side direction
  reversal or arbitrary truncation is evidenced.
- Inference: the overlap belongs to the allocation/request buffer contract or
  heap layout. Do not shorten the DMA as a workaround.
